## Facts read from the code (origin/main 3523b45)

- `shotAt` travel: position is the leaving pose (`exitPose`, drifting out at `rise.driftSpeed`) while `u < begin`, then the approach path of the next stop (`approachLocal`: the hold pose backed off along its view axis by `sigma`, logarithmic, `e = 1 - (1 - x)^power`). The next stop's origin is placed so both agree at `begin`. `begin` is when the leaving body, rings and moons are out of frame with 3 degrees to spare (`clearAt`), never before `travel.start` (0.2).
- Orientation: slerp from the leaving quaternion to the approach quaternion with power2.inOut over `u` in 0 to `turnEnd` (0.46 default). The slerp carries pan and roll together.
- The approach starts at its largest logarithmic rate at `begin` (`e'(0) = power`), so the camera's speed toward the next body steps from the drift to the maximum in one frame. Nothing shows it today except the dust.
- Dust: 2600 points in a 9 unit cube that wraps round the camera, `gl_PointSize`, one point per time sample (dots). Offset `approach * -28 * smoother(ramp(0.1, 1, u))`, subtracted in the shader, so motes move toward the next body: backward.
- The belt (`src/space/belt/motion.ts`) reads `shot.dustOffset` and the camera at the judged frame 2782 and a quarter frame each side of it. So `shot.dustOffset` must keep its present value in `mars.travel` or the rocks move. The dust gets its own field.
- Far bodies: stand-ins draw their own point sprite when the radius is under about 15 px (`src/bodies/standin.ts`), Mars uses `src/bodies/farpoint.ts`. Both are drawn once per time sample, so in a pan the point is a row of dots.
- The sky and stars are drawn at the middle of the shutter in every sample (stars stay points).

## Design decisions

- Dust: `shot.dustOffset` stays as it is (the belt reads it). The dust has its own flow: `src/space/travel/flow.ts` integrates the camera's flight direction times a logarithmic function of its real speed, at four fixed steps per frame from the first frame of the segment, cached per segment. Each mote is one instanced quad drawn from its screen position at shutter open to shutter close, energy divided by the streak's area, drawn the same in every time sample, so every tier gets the same smooth streak. Near motes get a circle of confusion (large faint discs). Lit by the Sun with a forward lobe. Amount is zero at both joins.
- Sub-pixel bodies: "sky lock" in `shotAt`: rotate the arriving set about the camera per time sample so the far point holds still on the sky like the stars do, fading out as the disc grows (3 to 40 px).
- Camera: turn spread over most of the segment with pan and roll decoupled, approach curve whose logarithmic rate starts at zero (no speed step at `begin`), own table in `src/space/travel/` that overrides each plan's `travel` and `arrive`, each of the eight its own side, swing and timing. `mars.travel` and `saturn.travel` keep their world rotation and both end directions.
- Ending: own sky aim switched under the `neptune.rise` cover, Milky Way behind the Sun, Neptune a lit crescent at the frame edge, a move that never stops, last frame black.
- 05:31 Milestone 1 (dust) reported to the Builder: commits 9bb0511, 5887f47, four Mars hashes match (out/work/travel/dust/), review still of 1078 20.0 s (before not measured).
- 05:32 Camera committed: 32254ec (path module), eb1daef (film.ts travel branch), 8a4921b (probes). `place()` and the legacy `clearAt`/`begin` are untouched, so every stop's world rotation and origin keep their bits and no turn changed. The new clear time (`clear[]`, from the new turn) replaces `begin` only inside the travel branch.

## Camera as built

- `src/space/travel/path.ts`: `TravelLook` per leaving stop (table `TRAVEL`, defaults `BASE`). `betaEase(a, b)` is the closed-form CDF of u^a (1-u)^b: zero rate at both ends, peak at a/(a+b). `TravelShape.turn(qa, qb, u)`: the view axis follows the great circle from the leaving to the arriving axis with the pan ease, plus a sideways bow (sin^2 of the pan, zero at both ends), and the roll is its own slerp between the two swing-transported orientations with the roll ease. `inFrame` is an exact frustum test.
- `film.ts`: `travelCamera(index, time)`: position = approach(x) + (leaving(time) - approach(0)) * settleLeft(x) with x = ramp(clear, 1, u); the approach uses `e = shape.grow(x)` so its logarithmic rate starts at zero. `clearOf(i, shape)`: first u at which the leaving body, rings and moons are out of frame under the new turn.
- Auto bow: if a travel's clear time with the table's bow is later than `clearBy` (0.34), the director tries bows from -24 to 24 in steps of 2 and takes the one nearest the table's bow whose clear time is within 0.02 of the best. Today that changes mars (bow 10 to -16, clear 0.41 to 0.30, Phobos no longer crosses the frame) and saturn (bow 8 to 20, clear 0.61 to 0.37, Titan no longer crosses). It follows the plans, no number copied.
- Sky lock (`LOCK_PX` 3 to 40 px of radius at 1080 lines): in a travel the arriving set is rotated about the camera, per time sample, by slerp(identity, q_sample * q_mid^-1, lock), so a far body holds still on the sky across the shutter like the stars do (they are drawn at the middle of the shutter). At one sample per frame nothing changes.
- Point phase: the grow and pan eases are set so the next body is in frame about 10 frames before it passes 10 px (mercury pan [2,5] grow [3,3], earth panSpan [0,0.9] grow [3,4], uranus pan [2,5] grow [4,3]).
- Draft sheets `out/work/travel/cam1/` (05:30): all eight read as turn away, open sky, point, disc, hero. sun.travel: the swap from the Sun set to the sky's disc happens in frames 477 to 488 (orange granules to a white disc), check it at review.
- 05:42 Review sheets `out/work/travel/cam2/` (mercury, saturn): fine. Review still 3927 (pan at 1.9 deg/frame, Uranus 1 px): with the sky lock the far point is one clean dot at its mid-shutter position. Dust at review read as thin sharp hairlines, all parallel in the pan (rain). Changed: core 0.85 + 0.9 seed px (was 0.62 + 0.6), extra falloff 1 / (1 + streak length / 24 px) so long pan streaks sink, `DUST.long`.
- Note for the report: commit 2fe2ec9 holds three topics in one commit (auto bow, sky lock, point phase). Keep later commits to one topic.

## Ending as built (05:50, uncommitted until the sheets are seen)

- `LOOKBACK` in `film.ts`, all values at the judged lookback frame (middle of `ending.lookback`), every rate constant in time so the move never stops, through the fade as well: `neptuneDeg 44` (angle Sun, camera, Neptune) growing 0.4 deg/s, `aimDeg 14` (the Sun sits 14 deg from the lens axis) falling 1.6 deg/s (0.055 deg/frame, about 1.7 px/frame), `distance 5.6` radii growing 1.5 percent/s, `rollDeg 13` growing 0.4 deg/s, `vfov 34`, `glare 3`, `flare 0.35`, `skyExposure 1`, `sky [0, 35, -5]` (along, tilt, lift: the galactic centre behind the Sun, band rising to the right).
- `lookback(t)` is the pose; the retreat blends from Neptune's exit pose into it as before, so the join retreat to lookback is continuous by construction.
- Sky: `aimSky()` adds `orbits[ending]`, fitted at the judged lookback frame. The ending uses it, and the orbit part of the last stop's rise (after its cover) uses it, the same rule every other stop follows for the next stop's sky.
- Measured: join rise to retreat turn 0.002 to 0.002, speed 1.01e-1 to 1.00e-1; retreat to lookback turn 0.055 to 0.055, fov 34, speed 8.88e-2 to 8.86e-2; lookback to fade 0.054 to 0.054. Retreat peak turn 1.61 deg/frame at 5084. Lookback turn 0.055 deg/frame all the way (was 0.03 falling to 0.01).
- Open: the engine draws a stop's sky patch with weight 1 in the ending kinds (`renderSample` in `engine.ts`: only `travel` and `rise` are special). With the real Neptune (it has a sky patch and its own aim) the patch would be drawn in the ending, pinned to a sky that has turned. That line is not the dust call, so it goes in `REQUESTS.md`.

## Sky task (Builder mail 2026-11-21 11:15 UTC): sky across the eight travels and the retreat

- Merged main 56b2ce9 into agent/travel (fast forward), build green. The harness renders from `dist` and every tool call rebuilds when the tree changed (`ensureBuild`), so never edit sources while a chain of renders is running: the next call in the chain picks the edit up.
- Byte baseline at draft before any change: `bash assets-src/travel/bytes.sh sky-before` (22 frames in `out/work/travel/sky-before/`, hashes in `/tmp/bytes-before.log`). After: `bash assets-src/travel/bytes.sh sky-after`, then cmp each pair.
- Cause of the black middle: `STAR_VERT` dims every star off the band by `crowd` (floor 0.14, about 0.31 after the power) times the stop's sky exposure (0.25 to 0.65). The band is only in view in earth.travel.
- Built (all numbers in `config/showcase.json` `travel.sky`):
  - `src/space/travel/open.ts`: `discShare` (share of the frame a disc covers, smooth) and `openSky`.
  - `film.ts`: `bright(index, time)` = leaving body share times its weight + arriving body share + the sky's Sun disc share; `brightNear` averages it over plus and minus `window` seconds; `openOf` = `1 - smoothstep(bright / ref)` with `ref = min(brightShare, bright one frame after the start, bright one frame before the end)`, so the first and last travel frame keep today's sky exposure exactly (measured with `npx tsx assets-src/travel/open.ts`: open 0 at both).
  - Travel sky exposure = mix(today's value, `openExposure`, open). New `Shot.skyStars` = open lifts the star floor (`Sky.setStarLift`, uniform `uStarLift` in the star vertex shader, factor exactly 1 when the lift is 0).
  - New `Shot.skyPatch`: the arriving patch weight. `patchSpanOf` scans the travel camera for the last time it is more than `patchSettleDeg` off its final direction; the patch eases in from there to `patchDone`. The patch feather (margin 12.5 percent of a 30 degree view) is the hold frame's border, so any earlier and the border is in frame.
  - Retreat: sky exposure eases over the whole retreat (`retreat`), stars lift with Neptune's lit share and close again from `retreatClose`, so the lookback is untouched.
- Engine files touched: `src/core/types.ts` (two optional Shot fields), `src/core/engine.ts` (patch weight from the shot, star lift call), `src/space/sky.ts` (uniform and setter).
- Builder feedback 11:42 on the first looks: middle still thin (wanted the density of earth.travel 2195 to 2222, thousands of points, a floor for the points that does not lift the glow, or more faint points in flight), uranus.travel pumps (4475 black, 4489 band at full, 4502 black), retreat still steps 5090 to 5101. Judge on a sheet, then on full size stills of 1632, 3352, 3922.
- Measured (pixels above code 40, scaled to an 800 by 450 crop, draft): earth.travel 2208 = 24672 (the model). 1632/3352/3922 with the star floor only = 2716/2796/2630. The catalog keeps 12 percent of its main stars off the band (`skyframe.json stars.floor`), so the floor alone cannot reach the model.
- Second build: a flight star layer (`Sky.setFlightStars`, 600000 points uniform over the sphere, own stream `flight stars`, child of the star points so it shares the sky turn, drawn only when the lift is above 0, level independent of the glow exposure). 3352 with 500000 points at 0.012 = 7101. Glow exposure in flight is its own level (`openExposure` 0.45) so the band does not swing; catalog stars are lifted to `starLevel` whatever the glow exposure.
- Arrival side: the open sky stays until the patch has taken over (`open = lens + (1 - lens) * (1 - patch) * ramp(arriveFrom)`), so there is no hole between the lens closing and the patch coming (was mars.travel 2806: Jupiter on black, then the patch in 6 frames). `patchSpan` 0.24 so the dissolve is at least 18 frames.
- Retreat: the band enters because the camera turns (5085 to 5101), not because of the exposure ramp. Glow now dips to `retreatGlow` by u 0.5 and rises to the lookback's 1 from there, stars are on their own level through the retreat, so the band enters dim and comes up to the end.
- Not mine, seen: mercury.travel end, Venus's patch paints a brown band across the centre while the engine band lies lower in the same view (two skies that do not agree, a dissolve can only cross them); uranus.travel 4522 to 4529 mirrored shapes right of Neptune are in Neptune's patch picture.
- State 12:30 UTC: branch at 938113c plus notes. Take sky6 (`out/work/travel/sky6/`): draft sheets of sun, mars, jupiter, saturn, earth, review sheets of venus, uranus, mercury, retreat, draft stills 1632 (10352), 3922 (10116), 5090, 5101. Byte check `sky-before` against `sky-after`: all 22 identical. The baseline frames from 3720 on were rendered after an unused config key and the not yet imported `open.ts` were in the tree, so they are checked again against a clean worktree of 56b2ce9 (`/tmp/pristine`, log `/tmp/pristine.log`).

## Travel task 3 (Builder mail 2026-11-21 13:30 UTC)

- Started 13:32. Merged main 5710e9f (engine batch 13 with the sky task on it), build green. Byte baseline of the 22 frames at draft in a clean worktree: `/tmp/bytes-t3base.log`, pictures `/tmp/base/out/work/travel/t3base/`.
- Render tree: `assets-src/travel/rt.sh stills|sheets|bytes ARGS` copies `src config assets-src tools` into the worktree `/tmp/base` and renders there, so the repo can be edited while a chain runs. One render process at a time still holds.
- New probes: `leave.ts` (leaving body across the shutter per travel), `tune.ts all | try ID JSON... | ID maxPx maxTurn lead weight` (limb speed across the 180 degree shutter in px, frames in view, peak turn, when the next body is in frame and when it passes 10 px), `bandtime.ts`, `band.ts` plus `band.py` (item 5), `fieldview.ts` plus `fieldview.py` (the flight field projected for six views without a render).
- Item 1, cause: at review the engine takes at most 64 time samples; Venus crossed the frame with its limb moving 77 px across the shutter (154 px a frame), the bright fine features inside the globe step by about 2 px a sample and stack into rows of dots and bars. Venus's set runs its own motion taps only in dive and rise (not mine), so the fix is the path.
- Item 1, built: `TravelLook.lead = [share, until]`: the pan is the sum of two eases, a slow first one that carries `share` of the turn by `until`, and the main one over `panSpan`. Share 0 is the old single ease exactly. Venus: `pan [2,4], panSpan [0.2,1], lead [0.25,0.35], rollSpan [0.2,0.96], clearBy 0.7` (the roll of 134 degrees starts only when the globe is almost out; it added much of the speed at the frame edge).
- Venus measured (tune.ts): limb 77.1 to 41.3 px across the shutter, in view 18 to 24 frames (1605 to 1628), the globe drifts out at a steady 62 px a frame from 1616 to 1627, peak turn 4.12 to 4.21 deg/frame (now at 1648, over stars), Earth in frame from 1645 at 0.44 px, 1 px at 1649, 10 px at 1656 (was 1637 and 1653). Slower trials (27 to 35 px) put Earth into the frame at 1651 to 1660 when it is already near 10 px, so they were dropped.
- Other travels, same measure on the old path (limb px across the shutter): mars 66.5, uranus 68.3, mercury 61.2, jupiter 60.9, saturn 59.2, sun 51.7, earth 51.3. First build looped over all lobes per pixel in the shader: over 5 minutes a review still, replaced by the baked map (35 s).

- Item 1. ## Task 6 (MAIL 46, Builder, 01:03 UTC on 2026-11-22): one band look for the film, and the judged travel frames

The mail, kept word for word where it binds:

"Your task 5 PART is received (aaae2d08). The task 5 sheets you still owe are not needed ... Work on top of aaae2d08, no rebase. It comes in as part of engine batch 17."

What the Builder saw (review tier, dba3ebc): "1. The Milky Way has three looks in one film: each stop's approved sky in its hold; a blue violet band with olive haze wherever the shared sky shows at a stop (rises, the lookback); a bright gold band in the middle of a travel. The change from one to the next happens in view within about six frames: 2236 to 2242, 3898 to 3905, 4468 to 4475, 5122 to 5144. 2. The band is gone within six frames where Saturn's plain approved sky comes in (3376 to 3382), and it comes up within seven frames at 1666 and at 2806. 3. Judged frames 1642 and 3922 are plain dark fields: no band, no next body. 4492 has the band but no next body. 1632 to 1645 and 3919 to 3932 are near empty at sheet size. 4. The retreat shows a gold band at 5085, the lookback a blue one. The cause as I read src/space/sky.ts on your branch: the warm grade of the band is mixed in by uFlightGlow.w, which is the flight lift, so the colour follows the exposure."

The decision: "(a) Colour and exposure are separated. The flight lift changes brightness only. (b) The band's look becomes a weight per stop between today's shared look (0) and the warm flight grade (1): colour and contrast, at the mean brightness the shared band has today, so no stop frame gets brighter by it. The weight is held through every segment of a stop. In a travel it moves from the leaving stop's weight to the arriving stop's, once, over at least twenty frames and never faster than the arriving sky patch comes in. The ending keeps Neptune's weight, so the retreat, the lookback and the fade show one band. (c) Commit 1 is the engine change with every weight at 0. With every weight at 0, every stop frame keeps the bytes it has on aaae2d08: your 22 frame draft list and the four Mars finals. Where the weight lives (a number per stop in the travel.sky block of the config, or a plan field with the default 0) is your choice; say which. (d) Commit 2, separate and with nothing else in it, sets the weight to 1 for every stop but Mars. Mars stays at 0: it is approved and its frames keep their bytes. I run the freshness look on commit 2 (every finished stop's judged stills, the rises too) and may set single stops back to 0, so the travels on both sides must carry any mix of 0 and 1 without a switch that can be seen. (e) The arriving sky patch comes in over at least twenty frames in every travel. Where the arriving patch is plain (Saturn) the band is not switched off: the patch fades in while the lift closes, and no run of frames shows the band vanishing or coming up in fewer than twenty frames (3376 to 3382, 1666, 2806). (f) The judged frame of every travel (495, 1072, 1642, 2212, 2782, 3352, 3922, 4492) shows open sky that is not an empty card: the next body in frame as a point of light with a small glint, as Venus is at 1072, and the band or a dense star field behind it. In venus.travel, saturn.travel and uranus.travel that means the turn settles earlier, so the next body is in frame from at least four frames before the judged frame. Do not change the camera path of mars.travel: the belt is fitted to it. (g) The lookback keeps the Sun as one bright star in the band with Neptune's thin crescent at the edge of frame."

"The kit's lines stay as they are: the leaving body leaves frame behind the camera and is not seen shrinking; the next body grows from a point into its hero framing by the start of its hold; the camera has mass, so every turn is eased and no join moves (the first and last frame of every travel keep their poses); stars do not streak; changes of exposure happen during travel, slowly."

Limits: "src/space/sky.ts, src/space/travel/, src/space/dust.ts, assets-src/travel/, the travel and ending blocks of src/director/film.ts, and the travel block of config/showcase.json. List every line you touch outside your folders. No stop folder: if a stop's plan would have to change, write that to me instead."

Proof and pictures: "After commit 1: the 22 frame draft list and the four Mars finals, all equal. After commit 2: review sheets of the eight travels and the three ending segments, and the finals of 495, 1072, 1642, 2212, 2782, 3352, 3922, 4492, 5085 and 5235. Look at every sheet yourself before you send it. Push looks as you go. Attach the eleven sheets as JPEG files (at most ten files per message, so two messages). If you are not done at 02:15 UTC, send a message that starts with PART with what stands, and carry on."

## 04:00 UTC MAIL 67 (binding parts verbatim)

"The veil dither is `fract(sin(dot(gl_FragCoord.xy, ...)) * 43758.5453)`. The last bit of the GPU's `sin` of a number near 1e5 decides its value, so it is the same on one machine every time but need not be the same on a worker with another CPU type or another browser build ... Replace it with an integer hash of the pixel, which gives the same bits everywhere, for example:
highp uint h = uint(gl_FragCoord.x) * 0x9E3779B1u ^ uint(gl_FragCoord.y) * 0x85EBCA77u;
h ^= h >> 15; h *= 0x2C1B3C6Du; h ^= h >> 12; h *= 0x297A2D39u; h ^= h >> 15;
float d = float(h >> 8) * (1.0 / 16777216.0);
(or the engine's own integer hash if the shared shader code already has one; say which you used). Still fixed to the screen, no frame number, same amplitude as now. The pattern changes, the look must not: render 1625 at the final tier once more after it and look at the darkest box at native size for any visible pattern (lines, a lattice, a diagonal weave). If this costs more than ten minutes, send the DONE first with what you have and say when the dither follows; I build the candidate only when both are in.
In the DONE, as asked in mail 64: the commit, the box numbers of 1625, 3912, 482 and 2772, where the pictures are (456 and 1920), the venus.travel sheet at review, how the points come up with the lift, the proof that hold, dive and terrain frames keep their bytes against today's main, and what your view list does at the frame edge (a bright point just outside the frame whose glow reaches into it must still be drawn)."

## 08:47 UTC: the Builder takes the pinned variant for all five; task list, due 09:45 (PART 09:20)

His words in short. "Decision: the pinned variant is taken, for all five travels (mercury, venus, earth, saturn, uranus). The committed whip is dropped." Four changes before DONE, in order of worth:
1. "No fly in. The point must not be seen coming up from the bottom edge and parking. Keep the geometry as it is, and make the far point's light zero until the body is within 3 px of its hold place, then let it rise from nothing to its full level over about 12 frames (smooth, no step over 15 percent of its full level a frame)." If the level curve lives in src/bodies/farpoint.ts, change it there for travels only and prove the ending (5085, 5235) and every hold first frame keep their bytes.
2. "No shrink at the hand over (2227 to 2229 on earth). Measure the apparent width of the body per frame (pixels at 8 levels or more above the sky along a row through its centre) from the first visible frame to the hold: it must never fall from one frame to the next." Cure in farpoint.ts: the glow narrows with the level so it is never wider than the disc plus its bloom at the hand over, or the glow stays over the young disc and fades out between 2 and 12 px of radius. 3. "A softer start of the turn." Inside the window the camera keeps the path's direction from the body and its distance is set so that the size on screen is `small + (size in the hold at this time - small) * smoothstep(t)`; `small` is the path's size where the window opens. Unpinned travels (sun; mars if its window is ever set) also turn the view ray onto the hold's own ray over the first 12 frames of the window, so Mercury sits in its hold place from 512 instead of sliding 250 px while it grows. With window 0 `travelCamera` returns `travelPath` and `until` is untouched: all 5400 shot hashes equal f4472a3.

Seen on main at review (label qm, crops x 1220 to 1920, y 0 to 420): 1048 Mercury's smeared globe in front of the Sun plate; 1052 Mercury's night side sits on the Sun (Mercury centre (1777, 93) r 308, Sun (1843, 87) r 326), corona and prominences round it; 1060 and 1064: the plate is gone and a flat, limb darkened, see through disc with stars in it stands in its place. Cause, two parts in `film.ts`:
  1. Mercury's set (which holds the Sun plate) is dropped at `clear` (u 0.32, frame 1059, when the globe has left the frame), while the Sun is in frame until about 1066 (disc) and 1069 (twice the disc).
  2. The sky's own Sun disc mixes radius 9.2 to 0.9 degrees and glare 0.012 to 0.9 from u 0.25 (frame 1054), so the flat disc brightens while in frame (glare 0.07 at 1060, 0.18 at 1065).
- Fix in proof (`src/director/film.ts` only, not committed yet): `SUN_HAND = { span: [0.25, 0.8], last: 0.6, reach: 2, behind: 1000 }`. Per travel `sunHand[i]` = 0.25, or if the leaving stop's Sun (within twice its radius) is in frame at u 0.25, the u at which it has left (stepped in eighths of a frame, capped 0.6). The mix of radius and glare starts at `sunHand` (exposure, sky, flare keep 0.25). The leaving set keeps weight 1 until `max(clear, sunHand)` when `sunHand` is over 0.25; after `clear` its origin is put 1000 units behind the camera (the camera jumps to the far path at `clear`, so the globe's far point would else flash in frame: measured (775, 628) at 1059 without this).
  - Measured: sunHand 0.25 for every travel but mercury (0.465, frame 1069.9); the Sun's disc is in frame in no other travel on the leaving side (uranus.travel has the far Sun 4509 to 4529 on the arriving side, untouched). Glare stays 0.012 and radius 9.2 through 1069, then 0.034 at 1072, 0.34 at 1080, 0.9 at 1096.

The travel's slide must start with no acceleration (speed AND acceleration continuous through the seam, acceleration growing from zero as the shape of the whole start), the same at the far end where the travel arrives at the hold, and the retreat past Neptune gets the same start (that block of shotAt is mine for this). Prove with `npm run smooth -- --stop ID --only path`: rows ENDFRAME + 1 and + 2 inside 0.08 for mercury, venus, earth, mars, uranus, neptune; quote the worst step of each travel's first and last 15 frames before and after; state what is still wrong; answer with a time (answered: pushed by 23:40 UTC).
- Three steps found, all three cured in one commit:
  1. START: `TravelShape.pass` was `t^p` (p 2 Mars, 3 the others): full acceleration (p 2) or full jerk (p 3) from the first frame. Now `passCurve(power, knee, lead, round)` in path.ts: the acceleration is `p (p - 1) t^(p - 2)` times a quintic ramp over `passLead` (share of passBy; 0.5, Uranus 0.6) and, at a knee, times a smoothstep fall over `passKnee - passRound` to `passKnee + passRound` (round 0.2); integrated twice in closed form as piecewise polynomials, divided by its value at t = 1. So pass(passBy) is still 1: slide, clear, from, until and open of every travel are the same numbers as before (printed both ways), only `small` moves in its fifth digit.
  2. KNEE: the old knee (mercury, venus, jupiter, uranus) dropped the acceleration to zero in one frame in mid slide (17 to 27 px a frame per frame). It is now the smoothstep fall above.
  3. FAR END: travelCamera grew the arriving planet with a smoothstep over the window: the edge slowed at a constant 1.6 to 2.4 px a frame per frame and stopped in one frame at the hold (and started with the same step when the window opened). Now `smoother` (quintic): no step at either end.
  4. RETREAT: the light direction of the retreat ran on `smooth(u * 1.5)`, full acceleration from the first frame (turn 0.039 of the mean at 5027). Now `smoother`. The glides (aim, phase, turn, pull) already start with no acceleration.

Each ends at exactly its exit's drift: `rise.driftSpeed` along `riseOut` (0.0800 radii a second, Earth 0.500), direction cosine 1.0000, gap of place under 2.1e-9 radii, turn under 4e-4 rad a second. "At rest" = the drift; the carry is the excess over it.
- INTERFACE sent to handover_engineer, copy to the Builder (my mail said 09:16, typed without reading the clock; it was about 09:10): place as today on `riseEnd`; a stated speed above the drift along the rise's own line; the rise may end in the middle of its brake (I carry the acceleration on); it does not speed up at the end nor switch its law in the last 1/300 s; turn of the sky and vfov at rest as today; `eyeShot('rise', t, 'orbit')` valid to the end, own path stops too. No field and no flag from his side. `exit()` stays his.
- BUILT: path.ts `CARRY = { from: 0.02, step: 1 / 600 }`, `carryWay(s)` (speed 1 - smoother(s), way 0.5 at 1), `carryBend(s)` (speed s (1 - s)^3, way 0.05 at 1), look field `carry` (seconds, BASE 0.4). film.ts `carryOf(i)` (once per leg: one sided velocity (3 p0 - 4 p1 + p2) / 2h minus the exit's own drift, acceleration (p0 - 2 p1 + p2) / h^2; under CARRY.from: null), `carried(i, time)` = `leaving` plus speed T carryWay(t/T) plus rate T^2 carryBend(t/T).
- WHERE the carry goes in, and why: `leaving` itself stays the exit, because `place` and `clearAt` put the next stop's world on it (carry there would move every later world). `carried` is used by `travelPath` (the camera), `passSeen` (the slide is solved anew so the leaving body is out of the frame by passBy; without it the body would still be in the frame when its set is put behind the camera) and `leavingThings`.
- Bounds: the carried speed never turns back while brake times T is at most 7.9 times the speed, and falls monotonically up to 7.5 times (worked out and tested).
- MEASURED with a stand in rise (the last 0.05 s of the rise replaced by exit plus riseOut (4 t - 4 t^2), so excess 4 radii a second, brake 8; probe out/work/tmp/carry.ts): on all seven legs clear the same to the bit, worlds moved by 0, the camera's direction the same over the whole leg (only its place moves), slide solved anew (venus 3.842 to 4.177, saturn 16.004 to 16.342). The landing is NOT to the bit: the camera's place differs by at most 1.3e-7 of the distance to the next body (Saturn's leg; Venus 1.3e-8), because `small` (the size at which the window opens) is read from the path and changes by 2e-5 of itself, and on Saturn's leg the settle ends at u 0.71, after the landing's start 0.68. Under 2e-4 px. Giving the offset back before the window opens was weighed and dropped: the window opens 2 to 20 frames after the body clears, too short for 0.7 radii without a visible breath of the leaving body.

- Branch `agent/travel-place` off main 5179b435, merged with main 64c4ccb4 (plumbing: `git merge-tree --write-tree --merge-base=<old main> HEAD origin/main`, then `commit-tree -p HEAD -p origin/main`, then `merge --ff-only`; a plain `git merge` says "unrelated histories" because every depth 1 fetch is a root here; the true parent is in `git cat-file -p origin/main`). Commits: 33245636 (key, refusal, record, test), merge 0ed918a9, f625158d (table of its own, refusal by reach).
- What it is: `src/space/travel/path.ts` `WORLD_ANSWER` (mercury corner, earth open, jupiter close, uranus open; all others least), `WORLD_MISS` 1 degree, `worldCosts` (open and close give the tries of the other branch the cost Infinity; branch = whether the angle to the next body grows with the try), `worldLead` (lead of the taken try over every other local minimum 3 tries away or more). `src/director/film.ts` place(): same cost law, same tie loop, then a throw when `travel.turnDeg` lies more than 1 degree outside what the end of the rise reaches and the stop is not `corner`; record `film.placed` (id, taken, missDeg, leadDeg, bareLeadDeg, opens).
